Fund Descriptions 
1. General Fund (1xx) – Accounts for revenue and expenses associated with the general operations of 
the District. 
2. Debt Retirement Fund (3xx) – Accounts for revenue and expenses associated with the District’s 
debt obligations. 
3. Capital Projects Fund (414) – Accounts for the bond proceeds and expenses associated with the 
District’s bond projects (not included in this presentation).  
4. Capital Maintenance Fund (499) – Accounts for the expenses associated with the District’s capital 
maintenance projects. 
5. Food Service Fund (520) – Accounts for revenue and expenses associated with the District’s food 
service program. 
6. TCC Grant Fund (529) – Accounts for the revenue and expenses associated with the District’s 
operations of the Troy Career Center 
7. Community Service Fund (530) – Accounts for revenue and expenses associated with the District’s 
fee for service programs including PAL, CARE and TOTS. 
8. Facility Rental Fund (535) – Accounts for the revenue and expenses associated with the District’s 
rental of various facilities.

2015-16 General Fund State Aid Revenue Budget 
Assumptions 
• Foundation Allowance:  increase of $70 per pupil = +$879,200 
• Enrollment assumption: 12,560 (increase of 5 students) = +$125 
• Categoricals: 
– Elimination of Best Practice Categorical: $50 per pupil =               
-$628,000 
– Elimination of Performance Incentive Categorical: $100 per 
pupil = -$1,256,000 
– Continuation and Increase of Offset Payments: $105 per pupil = 
$1,318,800 
• Net Impact: $25 per pupil x 12,560 students = $314,000

2015-16 General Fund Expenditure Budget 
Assumptions 
• Salary Increase of approximately 2% 
• Benefit Increases 
– Health Insurance: 2.3% increase in PA 152 hard cap rate 
– Retirement Increase to reflect increase of salaries and 2.9% increase in MPSERS 
UAAL rate stabilization (7.63% to 10.53%) 
– FICA increase to reflect increase of salaries 
• No significant changes in Purchased Services, Supplies, and Other Expenditures 
• Capital Outlay decreased by 53% as we assumed no TRIG grant expenditures. 
• Outgoing Transfer and Other Transaction were reduced as a result of closing the  closed 
the Durant Fund 
• *Budget reduction plan will be developed to ensure a 12% fund balance at the end of

TROY SCHOOL DISTRICT 
2015-16 PROPOSED  
GENERAL FUND BUDGET ASSUMPTIONS    
 The projected blended student count of 12,560.00 is a slight increase from 2014/15. The 
pupil membership formula changed in 2013/14 to use current fiscal year fall (90%) and 
spring (10%) counts.  Previously, the prior fiscal year spring count was used. 
signature would increase our foundation allowance $70 per pupil to $8,955.  The net 
impact given the reduction in categorical funding is $25 per pupil. 
 Reduction of the Best Practice Categorical ($50 per pupil) and the Performance Incentive 
Categorical ($100 per pupil).   
budget. 
Salary & Benefit Items 
 The retirement rate is budgeted with an increase from 33.41% to 36.31% on October 1, 
2015.  The effective retirement rate budgeted is 25.78%. The new higher rate will be 
 The FICA rate of 7.65% has not changed. 
the State mandated premium caps. The limits for 2015 equal the 2014 limits increased by 
 We continue work to adjust Salary and Benefits based on information to date.  Overall, 
the assumption is a net increase of 2% in salaries.  The following is a status of the 
employee groups: 
 Teachers (TEA)  
Salaries include a one (1) step movement on the current 14 step schedule along with 
board paid tax sheltered annuity payment of $1,125 
 Secretarial (TESA) 
Salaries will include elimination of the 4% off-schedule payment from 2014/15; 2% 
across-the-board improvement to the wage schedule; 2 half-step improvements for all 
eligible employees; and 3% off-schedule payment to those not benefiting from step 
advancement.    

 The current foundation allowance is $8,885.00 per pupil.  The bill sent to the Governor for 

 MPSERS Categorical changes are expected to have no net impact on the bottom-line 
offset by a State funding element called “UAAL Rate Stabilization”.   

 Employee benefit costs for health, dental, vision, life and disability are estimated based on 
2.3%.

 Support Personnel (TESPA) 
Salaries will include elimination of the 4% off-schedule payment from 2014/15; 1% 
across-the-board improvement to the wage schedule; one step improvements for all 
who are currently on step 5 or higher.  
 Administrators  
A one (1) step movement will be given to those administrators eligible for movement on 
their current wage schedule. 
 Non-Represented 
A one (1) step movement will be given to those non-represented employees eligible for 
movement on their current wage schedule. 
Other Non-salary Items –  
 Purchased services, supplies and materials, capital outlay, and other were not 
significantly changed from the 2014-15 final amendment.  
 The General Fund is receiving a transfer of $650,000 from the Community Service 
Fund  
 Budget reductions in the amount $3,000,000 will be made to ensure a 12% fund 
balance is maintained for the 2015/16 fiscal year.

Fiscal Year 2015-16 Grants 
GENERAL FUND GRANTS 
Adult Education Civics ($18,000) – Federal funds to support projects that demonstrate 
effective and innovative practices in providing and increasing access to programs and services 
Adult Education Family Literacy ($115,000) – Federal funds to meet the extemporaneous 
needs of the adult education faculty to upgrade their skills and competencies. 
IDEA-Flowthrough ($1,952,281) – Federal funds to provide the instructional services for 
outlined in the IEP. 
IDEA-Preschool ($58,996) – Federal funds to provide services for “at risk” 3-6 year old 
preprimary impaired children and their parents. 
four year old students and their parents. 
CTE Millage ($119,964) – County millage funds to support career and technical education 
programs. 
providing instructional services to eligible K-8 students in reading, writing, science, math and 
social studies; provides parental involvement support; and instructional support for homeless 
students who attend the Troy School District. 
Title II, Part A, Teacher/Principal Training ($197,127) – Federal funds to implement 
smaller class sizes in grades K-3 and to implement professional development opportunities in 
Title III, English Language Acquisition Program ($143,468) – Federal funds to support 
English language learners from Pre-Kindergarten through 12th grade. 
TROY CAREER CENTER GRANTS 
Workforce Investment Act ($1,959,993) – Federal funds to provide employers and youth 
and adult job seekers with seamless, integrated access to all workforce development 
programs and services operated by the Oakland County Michigan Works! Service Centers. 
that integrate English literacy and civics education. 
students with learning disabilities, emotional impairments or other eligibility designations as 
Michigan School Readiness ($222,166) – State funded preschool program to serve “at risk” 
Title I ($489,655) – Federal funds to help disadvantaged students meet high standards by 
the district to support building level, district level and curricular student achie
vement goals.

                 3. A.    06.23.15 
3.       RECOGNITION 
A. Superintendent’s Advisory Board 
This year, out of a diverse and impressive pool, 23 students from all four 
high schools were chosen as the inaugural members of the 
Superintendent’s Student Advisory Board. This group worked together to 
develop a mission statement and goal to make the Troy School District a 
better place for all students. They worked diligently and collaboratively all  
year to lend a student perspective on important issues and provided insight 
on the student experience. 
Though our graduating seniors on the Board will move on to colleges and 
universities this fall, we are hoping to see our sophomores, juniors and 
new seniors back on the Board next year when they begin to put some of 
their plans into action. 
Tonight, we are please d to recognize the following students for their 
service, their willingness to give of their time and talents and their 
dedication to the goals they created.  
Would the following members of the Superintendent’s Student Advisory 
Board please come forward as your name is read?  
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Present certificates

                 3. B.    06.23.15 
3.       RECOGNITION 
B. School Life Newspaper 
Tonight, we are pleased to honor Raad Alawan and Ed Morykwas  for their 
hard work and commitment to spreading the good news of the Troy School 
District through School Life Troy. We entered into a partnership with 
School Life this year and the feedback from parents, staff and students has 
been very positive. Each mon th, our students and staff are showcased for 
their achievements, charitable endeavors and activities inside and outside 
school. Raad is the publisher of School Life Troy and spends a great deal of 
time in the district to gather content and ensure the smooth running of this 
newspaper. Ed is the photographer for School Life and had captured and 
memorialized many wonderful moments this past year. From the first issue 
last September to the graduation issue a couple of weeks ago, School Life 
Troy has been a wonderful addition to the district and one that we hope is 
here for many years to come. 
Would Mr. Alawan and Mr. Morykwas please come forward at this time? 
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Present certificates

5. A.     06.23.15 
BOARD OF EDUCATION 
TROY, MICHIGAN 
BOARD MEETING   MINUTES   MAY 5, 2015 
       SERVICES BUILDING  
  President  Philippart  called  the  meeting  to  order  at  7:00 
p.m. 
In  addition  to  Dr.  Philippart,  present  were  Board 
members Edmunds, Fleming, Hauff, Miletti, Schmidt, and   
Underwood.   Also  present  were  Superintendent 
Machesky;  administrators  Kerry  Birmingham,  Janice 
Brzezinski, Brian Canfield, Jerry Cottone, Tammy DiPonio, 
Mark  Dziatczak,  Tim  Fulcher,  Mary  Haezebrouck,  Kathy 
Jagels,  Matt  Jansen,  Larry  Lobert,  Audra  Melton,  Anne 
Mull, Shawna Veit, Jeremy Whan, Rick West, Kris Young; 
and approximately 150 members of the audience.  
RECOGNITION  
Mission Moment – Schroeder 
the initiative Schroeder parents and students started.  He 
explained  how  Schroeder  Elementary  has  teamed  up 
with  IA‐East  and  an  organization  called  buildOn  to  help 
build  schools  in  underprivileged  countries.  This  year, 
Schroeder is helping to build a school in Haiti. Schroeder 
students have also helped build schools in Nicaragua and 
Nepal. To make this project possible, Schroeder organizes 
an  annual  Kids  4  Kids  Sale  to  help  raise  money  for 
building  a  new  school.  Mr.  Canfield  introduced  the  two 
Schroeder parents, Mrs. Weallans and Mrs. Pingree, who 
organize  and  chair  the  event.  Schroeder  students 
participate by making numerous donations such as toys, 
athletic equipment, DVD’s, games and many other gently 
used  items.  Parents,  students  and  community  members 
are  invited  to  shop  at  the  sale,  which  is  one  of 
Schroeder’s most popular events! All of the money raised 
at  the  Kids  4  Kids  Sale  is  donated  to  buildOn.  Students 
from IA‐West travel to the designated country to join the 
construction crew and actually participate in building the 
new  school.  Every year,  IA  students  return  to  Schroeder 
and visit classrooms to show pictures of the new school. 
This  wonderful  event  helps  show  how  our  elementary 
students  can  change  the  world  and  help  those  in  need.  
The  two  parents  introduced  students  Britta  Pingree, 
Seeun Lee, Isabella Cook, and Jack Weallans who shared 
more about their initiative.

ELD Instructional Materials 
the  World‐Class  Instructional  Design  and  Assessment 
(WIDA)  consortium  in  January  of  2013.  WIDA  Standards 
have  an  explicit  connection  to  Michigan  State  Standards 
and are the stepping stones for helping students.  Due to 
the  District  and  our  growing  English  Learner  population, 
it  has  been  recommended  the  District  procure  new 
instructional  materials.   Dr.  Machesky  introduced  ELD 
Specialists, Ms. Christin Silagy and Ms. Danielle Andresen, 
who  shared  a  PowerPoint  presentation  to  introduce  the 
new curriculum being recommended.

MEMBER DEVELOPMENT OPPORTUNITIES    B‐2000 
B‐2002  COMPENSATION AND EXPENSES 
Rate of compensation for Board members shall be $100 per annum. 
Members on school district business will be reimbursed for actual 
and necessary expenses incurred in the discharge of official duties 
Board, by a majority vote of its members at an open meeting, 
approved reimbursement of the specific expense before the expense 
was incurred, or (2) the expense is consistent with a policy adopted 
by the Board establishing specific categories of reimbursable 
expenses, and the Board approves the reimbursement before it is 
actually paid administrative guidelines for reimbursable expenses.  
Each member is responsible for submitting an itemized statement 
of reimbursable expenses to the assistant superintendent—business 
services for listing with the regular monthly bills.

7. A.  PERSONNEL 
CONSIDERATION:  Administrative Appointment –  
     Director of Student Growth, Equity & Assessment 
I am pleased to recommend the appointment of  Ms. Jennifer Gottlieb  to the 
position of Director of Student Growth , Equity & Assessment .  Ms. Gottlieb 
comes back to Troy from Macomb Intermediate School District where she has been 
the Science Consultant for the past two years. Prior to MISD, Ms. Gottlieb spent two 
years as the Science Coordinator with Birmingham Public  Schools and 15 years with 
Troy Schools: 3 years as the Science and Math Coordinator and 12 years as a science 
teacher at Athens High School. Ms. Gottlieb received her Bachelor’s degree from the 
University of Michigan in 1989, her Master’s degree from Teac hers College, Columbia 
University in 1990 and her Education Specialist degree from Oakland University in 
2011. 
With your approval, Ms. Jennifer Gottlieb will begin her duties as Director of Student 
Growth, Equity & Assessment effective July 1, 2015. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Acceptance of the resolution below:  
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by         
 Supported by        
BE IT THEREFORE RESOLVED  that the Board of Education issues a two -year 
probationary administrative contract without tenure in the admi nistrative position to 
Ms. Jennifer Gottlieb, who will begin her duties as Director of Student Growth, Equity 
& Assessment, effective July 1, 2015.

7. B. PERSONNEL 
 CONSIDERATION:  SUPERINTENDENT’S CONTRACT EXTENSION  
The Board recently completed Superintendent Dr. Rich Machesky’s first year 
performance evaluation and would like to share the f ollowing assessment with 
the community: 
Dr. Richard Machesky has completed his first year as a HIGHLY EFFECTIVE 
superintendent and has met or exceeded expectations in all evaluation 
categories.  Even though this is his first year as superintendent, he has  
articulated a clear vision for the district and launched the concept of One Troy to 
create a culture of collaboration and motivate staff to take risks in advancing 
student achievement and district effectiveness.  Dr. Machesky has introduced a 
customer ser vice mindset into Central Office and has surrounded himself with 
skilled administrators to support the learning development of our kids.    Under 
his leadership several educational initiatives have been implemented or 
expanded, with a focus on ensuring all  students have the opportunity to learn.  
He has spent countless hours in the community, engaging parents, students, 
staff as well as community and business organizations listening, collecting 
feedback and advocating for our schools.    Under his leadership, the Troy School 
District has continued to thrive; the district remains fiscally strong, all labor 
contracts have been successfully negotiated, staff positions have been filled with 
outstanding talent and student opportunity and achievement continue to 
increase.  It has been a year of progress.  As a result, Dr. Machesky, consistent 
with other TSD staff is eligible for performance based compensation this year.  
Due to his exemplary performance and HIGHLY EFFECTIVE evaluation, the Board 
has authorized a $5000 bonus to be paid to Superintendent Machesky.     
It is also appropriate at this time for the board to exten d Dr. Machesky’s 
contract.  Consistent with the average total compensation increases for other 
staff groups, Superintendent Machesky’s contract will reflect a 3.5% base salary 
increase.  His contract will be extended through Jun 30, 2017.  
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Acceptance of the resolution below: 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION

7. E.  PERSONNEL 
 CONSIDERATION:  TESA Contract Ratification 
A tentative agreement between representatives of the Troy Board of Education and 
the Troy Educational Secretaries Association /MEA/NEA ( TESA) was reached on 
June 18th, 2015, and the TESA membership voted to ratify the agreement on June 
22nd, 2015.  The new one (1) year contract is for the period commencing July 1, 
2015 and ending June 30, 2016.  The agreement includes a 2% across-the-board 
improvement to the wage schedule, two step movement for eligible employees 
based upon the 21-step schedule in the agreement, a 3% off-schedule payment for 
those not benefiting from step movement, the elimination of the $100/$200 health 
insurance premium deductible payments identified in Appe ndix 16 of the 13/15 
agreement and the elimination of t he 4% off -schedule payment in the former 
agreement.  
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Accept the resolution below. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by: 
 Supported by: 
Be it therefore resolved that the Board of Education hereby ratifies and adopts 
the tentative agreement reached on June 18th, 2015, between representatives of 
the Troy Board of Education and the Troy Educational Secretaries 
Association/MEA/NEA for the period of July 1, 2015 through June 30, 2016.

9. A.      06.23.15 
9.  BUSINESS 
    A.  CONSIDERATION:  MILLAGE RENEWAL 
The School District’s operating millages  are set to expire at the end of 
2015.  In order to receive the full guaranteed foundation allowance per 
membership pupil, we are required to levy millage locally on all property, 
except property exempt by law.  The School District will hold a Special 
School E lection on November 3, 2015 to request approval of operating 
millage rates.  The official ballot language is submitted for Board of 
Education approval. 
____________________________________________________________ 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Acceptance of the resolution below. 
                SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
Moved By: ______________________________ 
Supported By: ______________________________ 
 WHEREAS, the School District is a Michigan general powers school district 
operating under the Revised School Code, as amended, and as such, the School 
District’s elections are governed by the Michigan Election Law, Public Act 116 of 1954, 
as amended (the “Michigan Election Law”); 
 WHEREAS, the School District will hold a Special School Election on Novembe r 
3, 2015, which Special School Election will be conducted by the Clerk of the County of 
Oakland;  
 WHEREAS, the Michigan Election Law requires that the School District certify the 
ballot language for any proposals to be voted on at a permitted election date to the School 
District’s Election Coordinator not later than 4:00 p.m., on the twelfth Tuesday before 
election date; 
 WHEREAS, Public Act 336 of 1993 amended the State School Aid Act by 
guaranteeing to each local school district a base “foundation al lowance” per membership 
pupil;  
300
 WHEREAS, in order to receive the full guaranteed foundation allowance per 
membership pupil, the School District is required to levy millage locally on all property, 
except  property exempted by law; 
 WHEREAS, the previously authorized operating millages of the School District are 
set to expire on December 31, 2015; 
 WHEREAS, the School District has determined that it will be necessary to continue 
the operating millage authorizations to provide for the operating needs of t he School 
District; 
 WHEREAS, the School District also desires to request an increase to the operating 
millage authorization assessed against all property, except property exempted by law, in 
order to restore the portion of the operating millage lost as a  result of the reduction 
required by the Michigan Constitution (i.e., the Headlee rollback) and to protect against 
future reductions. 
 WHEREAS, the School District desires to submit a proposal to approve the 
continuation and increase of the operating mill age authorizations at a Special School 
Election to be held on November 3, 2015; and 
 WHEREAS, the School District desires to approve the above referenced operating 
millage proposal and to authorize the Superintendent or his designee to certify the ballot 
language for the operating millage proposal to the School District’s Election Coordinator 
for the Special School Election to be held on November 3, 2015. 
 NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE BOARD OF EDUCATION OF THE 
TROY SCHOOL DISTRICT, COUNTY OF OAKLAND, MICHIGAN THAT: 
 1. The “Operating Millage Proposal”, attached hereto as Exhibit A, is hereby 
approved.   
 2. The Superintendent or his designee is authorized to certify the above 
referenced Proposal to the School District’s Election Coordinator on or before August 11, 
2015. 
 3. The Superintendent or his designee is furthered authorized to take any and 
all action required under the Michigan Election Law with regard to the School District’s 
Special School Election. 
 4. All resolutions and parts of resolutions insofar as they conflict with the 
provisions of this resolution are hereby rescinded.

 9. A.   06.23.15 
TROY SCHOOL DISTRICT 
COUNTY OF OAKLAND 
STATE OF MICHIGAN 
OPERATING MILLAGE PROPOSAL 
This proposal, if approved by the electors, will allow the Troy 
School District to continue to levy the number of operating 
mills required for the School District to receive revenues at the 
full per pupil foundation allowance permitted by  the State of 
Michigan. 
Shall the limitation on the total amount of taxes which may be assessed against 
all property, except principal residence and other property exempted by law, 
situated within the Troy School District, County of Oakland, State of Michigan, be 
increased as pro vided in the Michigan Constitution, in the amount of 18.3795 
mills with 18 mills being the maximum allowable levy ($18.00 on each $1,000 of 
taxable valuation), and against all principal residences and other property not 
exempted by law, by 5.99 mills ($5.9 9 on each $1,000 of taxable valuation), 
both millages to be for a period of nine (9) years, 2016 to 2025, inclusive with 
17.8795 mills of the above 18.3795 mills and 5.99 mills being a renewal of 
authorized millages which would otherwise expire on December  31, 2015 and 
0.1205 mills of the above 18.3795 mills being a restoration of millage lost as a 
result of the reduction required by the Michigan Constitution? These operating 
millages if approved and levied, would provide estimated revenues to the School 
District of Thirty Three Million Five Hundred Eighty Three Thousand 
($33,583,000) Dollars during the 2016 calendar year, to be used for general 
operating purposes.

Attached is the Budget Amendment Resolution for the 2014 - 15 fiscal year.  It 
includes the General Fund, Special Revenue Funds, Debt Service Funds, 2014 
Capital Projects Fund and the Capital Maintenance Fund.  Approval of the attached 
resolution is recommended.
We do not have to read the Budget Amendment Resolution in its entirety.  
However, it must be made a part of the official minutes.

           C.    CONSIDERATION:       2015-2016 Budget Appropriations Resolution

General Fund, Special Revenue Funds, Debt Service Funds, 2014 Capital Projects 
Fund and the Capital Maintenance Fund as required by the Department of Education.  
Approval of the attached resolution is recommended.

it must be made a part of the official minutes.

9.C.         6-23-15
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that for operating purposes 17.8795 mills be levied on
non-homesteads and 4.9918 mills be levied on homesteads and industrial personal property;
and, that 10.9918 mills be levied on commercial personal property; and that 4.70 mills 
be levied on all classes of property for debt retirement purposes.
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that no Board of Education member or employee of
the school shall expend any funds or oblige the expenditure of any funds except pursuant
to appropriations made by the Board of Education in keeping with the budgetary policy
statement hitherto adopted by the Board.  Changes in the amount appropriated by the 
Board shall require approval by the Board.
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the Superintendent is hereby charged with general 
supervision of the execution of the budget adopted by the Board and shall hold the
department heads responsible for performance of their responsibilities with the amount
appropriated by the Board of Education in keeping with the budgetary policy statement
hitherto adopted by the Board.
This Appropriation Resolution shall take effect immediately.

D.  CONSIDERATION:  Roof Assessment Management Program 
An RFP was issued to obtain competitive bids for Roof Assessment and 
Consulting Services.  Bid responses were reviewed by Mr. Rob Carson, 
Director of Maintenance and Operations and Michelle Ke rns, Owners 
Representative, and it is recommended the bid be awarded as stated 
below.  
 RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Accept the resolution below. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by:   
 Supported by: 
 Be it therefore resolved  that the bid for roof assessment and 
consulting be awarded to WeatherTech Consulting Group, Inc.  in the 
amount of $ 99,128.00 and that this amount be approved as an 
expenditure of the 2014 Capital Projects Fund.
